## AXIS-1: SRA D1 use-class lattice fit

Question: Does PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0 fit within SRA D1 use-class lattice for eee local-runtime CLI-binary-use?

1. Is PolyForm Noncommercial structurally similar to AGPL-3.0 (local-CLI-use OK per SRA D1) OR is it a distinct license class?

C:\Users\42\AppData\Local\Temp\claude-plugins-official-license-probe\plugins\cwc-makers\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:147:`esptool` is GPLv2+ and is intentionally **not** vendored — keeping the repository cleanly Apache-2.0 means the GPL bits live in the user's pip-managed environment, not in the tree. The skill's preflight checks for an importable `esptool` and, if missing, prompts to install it (`python -m pip install --user esptool` — `--user` dropped inside a venv so it lands in site-packages). For subprocess calls we use `[sys.executable, "-m", "esptool", ...]`; the subprocess inherits user-site so the pip-installed module imports cleanly. `requirements.txt` declares this for explicit setup; the prompt path is the default for first-time attendees who haven't run pip yet.

That makes it stricter than the current .mcp.json wording if read as only blocking SaaS resale or distribution. But the stated eee use class is local, personal/research/experimental, not commercial, not hosted for third parties, and not distributed as a product. The GitNexus license expressly permits any noncommercial purpose and personal research/experiment/testing uses, so current npm-global CLI/MCP binary use is acceptable with disclosure and attribution. Wave 102/103 did not explicitly codify PolyForm, so Wave 112 was an extension, not a direct precedent. The extension is sound under SRA D1 because SRA requires use-class precision before license rejection and already says the eee baseline makes most nonpermissive licenses acceptable with disclosure. Anthropic D7 does not independently ratify PolyForm; official docs and the plugin directory defer to per-plugin trust and license review.
